Decided: **a FIFO queue is received one message per call**, so SQS blocks the group while it runs (a batch may carry several of one group, which would run at once). 2c done: `queue-adapters/sqs.ts` (long poll 20 s, up to `maxInFlight` per receive or 1 for FIFO, visibility 60 s renewed every 20 s, delete on success, visibility 0 on failure and on stop, the client closed 30 s after a reset); `SqsRunMessage` and `KafkaRunMessage` in engine types (`RunInput.message` is a union); `test/sqs-consumer.test.ts` (8: a run per message and delete, failure then redrive to the DLQ, a run outlasting the visibility, FIFO order with groups alongside, `maxInFlight`, a lost delete, handover on stop, a missing queue on the trigger's state then picked up); stable over 3 repeats with Pub/Sub's. 3a done. **The spike:** `@confluentinc/kafka-javascript` 1.11.0 (librdkafka 2.16, prebuilt for darwin-arm64 and linux glibc, features ssl, sasl_plain, sasl_scram, sasl_oauthbearer) and `kafkajs` 2.2.4 both talked to `apache/kafka:4.1.0`; kafkajs warns on Node 24 (negative timeouts) and has had no release since 2023; Confluent's loads in `node:24-bookworm-slim` and its low-level `KafkaConsumer.consume(n)` matches the adapter's `pull(max)`: **chosen**. Its install script downloads the binary from GitHub (node-pre-gyp, falling back to a source build): it is in `onlyBuiltDependencies`. Built: `kafka/config.ts` (brokers, auth none|plain|scram-sha-256|scram-sha-512|mtls|aws-msk-iam, tls, username, CA, client certificate, clientId, the AWS identity's fields for MSK IAM; secrets password, clientKey, secretAccessKey; `kafkaSettingsProblem`, `rdkafkaSettings`), `kafka/client.ts` (`kafkaClient`: librdkafka settings, an admin and a producer connected on first use, **librdkafka's error log lines kept, because a wrong password or untrusted certificate comes back only as "Broker transport failure"**: `kafkaError` quotes the most telling line; `withSignal`), `kafka/connector.ts` (Test connection lists topics; the **produce** query answers each partition's first offset, the KafkaJS-style send reports no per-message offsets), `kafka/test-kafka.ts` `startTestKafka` (a KRaft broker with PLAINTEXT, SASL_SSL (PLAIN, SCRAM-256/512 users made by `kafka-storage format --add-scram`) and SSL with client auth listeners, advertised on 127.0.0.1; certificates are 100-year fixtures in `test/fixtures/kafka`). The server checks Kafka settings on save. 3b done: `kafka/msk-iam.ts` `mskIamToken` (`@smithy/signature-v4` 5.7.5 with a SHA-256 on node:crypto; GET `kafka.<region>.amazonaws.com/?Action=kafka-cluster:Connect` presigned for `kafka-cluster`, 900 s or less when the credentials expire sooner, `User-Agent=optiply-workflows`, base64url; `lifetime` absolute ms); `kafkaResourceClient` gives MSK IAM resources the `oauthbearer_token_refresh_cb` and names the AWS identity through STS. **Checked against AWS's own `aws-msk-iam-sasl-signer-js` 1.0.3** (a dev dependency): at the same signing moment the URLs match parameter for parameter, signature included; and the test broker's SASL listener now takes OAUTHBEARER (Kafka's unsecured validator), so a test proves librdkafka calls the raw callback through the KafkaJS-style API and connects with its token. A real MSK cluster stays unchecked (§12). 3c done (not split): `kafka/consumer.ts` `kafkaConsumer` (librdkafka's own `KafkaConsumer`: no auto-commit, `rebalance_cb: true` with the `rebalance` event to commit revoked partitions first, `consume(max, waitMs)`, `commit`/`commitNow`, pause/resume, fatal errors kept); `queue-adapters/kafka.ts` (a background poll loop so long runs never stop polling, which would drop the member after `max.poll.interval.ms`; a buffer of 25 per in-flight slot, partitions paused past it; per-key queues within a partition, keyless ones free; retries offered again after `retryBackoffSeconds·2^(n−1)` (≤ 900 s) and run as attempt n+1 through the shared `startDelivery`; after `maxAttempts` produced to the dead-letter topic with the `ow-*` headers, or dropped; a dead letter that fails is sent again later without another run; offsets committed up to the lowest unsettled every 5 s, on revoke and on stop; `reset` leaves and rejoins). `QueueMessage.input` may be a function of the attempt (Kafka's `startTrigger.message.attempt`); `backoffSecondMs` for tests. **Fixed on the way:** the Kafka client kept a disconnected producer after `close()`. `test/kafka-consumer.test.ts` (5, against the container): order per key with others alongside and the commit, retry then dead letter with headers and the key moving on, drop without a topic, handover on stop without a second run or a lost message, `maxInFlight`; passed twice. 4 done: `workflows.triggers` gives every queue trigger (Pub/Sub, SQS, Kafka) `queue: {worker, state, lastDay: {messages, succeeded, failed (incl. dead-lettered and dropped), deadLettered}}` (was `pubsub`; the web reads `queue`); releases tests on a real worker: SQS (fake AWS) and Kafka (container) messages run the live release, defaults projected (Kafka's group `ow-<workflow id>-<name>`), the editor's state seen. Releasing needing Use on trigger resources, run inputs and the triggers' state needed nothing new. 5a done: `pages/resources/queue-settings.tsx` (`AwsIdentityFields`: region, access key or this server's credentials from `resources.awsIdentity`, role and external id, locked for editors where an admin chose the server's; `SqsFields` with the endpoint for admins only; `KafkaFields`: brokers one per line, sign-in, TLS, SASL user and password, client certificate and key, CA, client id, MSK IAM's AWS fields); `lib/resource-types.ts` `MessageKind`, `MESSAGE_QUERIES`, `defaultMessageQuery`, badges SQS (red) and KF (purple); the workflow inspector's `MessageQueryForm` for all three kinds (the kind guessed from the query when its resource is gone); the playground's `MessageDraft`. Apps edit SQS and Kafka queries as JSON, like Pub/Sub's. 5b done: the triggers form's `QueueSection`/`QueueCard` for Pub/Sub, SQS and Kafka (`QUEUES` per kind; Pub/Sub's test ids kept; Kafka's topics, group, start, attempts, backoff and dead-letter topic); `lib/runs-api.ts` `queueMessageLine` (runs list and the run panel's `run-queue`; Pub/Sub's `run-pubsub` text unchanged); `scopeDeclarations(…, queues)` types `startTrigger.message` as the workflow's kinds (`OwSqsMessage`, `OwKafkaMessage`; Pub/Sub's without any). E2E setup: a fake AWS (keys in `E2E_AWS_KEY_ID`/`E2E_AWS_SECRET`, role `E2E_AWS_ROLE` with external id `optiply-e2e`, queues `orders`, `m26`, `m26-flaky` → `m26-dlq` after 2, `m26-slow` 2 s, `m26-groups.fifo`, `m26-busy`, `m26-sent`; the server's own AWS credentials are a fake user's through `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID`) and, with Docker, a Kafka container (`E2E_KAFKA`, `E2E_KAFKA_SCRAM`; topics per part); control routes `/sqs/send`, `/sqs/messages`, `/kafka/produce`, `/kafka/read`; helpers `fakeSqs`, `testKafka`. `e2e/queue-triggers.spec.ts` (4: SQS resource with a key, the server's credentials offered, the playground's send; Kafka resource with SCRAM over TLS and the CA, the playground's produce; an SQS trigger and a Kafka trigger set up in the editor run per message, counted, named on the run pages) passed, with `pubsub.spec.ts`. 6a done: `apps/web/e2e/m26-done-when.spec.ts` (2 tests, a step per done-when item: SQS a run per message and its delete, a failure handed back and the redrive to the DLQ, a run outlasting the visibility, FIFO order with a group alongside, `maxInFlight`, a send query starting a trigger, an assumed role and the server's credentials, a member refused them, the secret never in a response; Kafka order per key with another key alongside, retries and the dead-letter topic with its headers while the key moves on, a produce query starting a trigger, SCRAM over TLS and mTLS, the password and key never in a response) passed first time; M19's done-when passed again (it read the triggers query's `pubsub` field, now `queue`: fixed). Full e2e 126 passed, 1 failed (`m22-done-when` under load; alone, passing). Review (code-review high): 10 found, all fixed, `a6b2214`. 6b: the image `optiply-workflows:m26-candidate` built and checked (not tagged `local`). 6c: docs (runbooks `sqs.md`, `kafka.md`, Pub/Sub's updated, `docker-compose.aws.yml`, `docker-compose.kafka.yml`, `.env.example`, §9u, §10, §12 items 33–35, §13 "From M26", architecture "As built"). **Left for the user:** rebuilding their Compose stack on M26 (ask first), then trying SQS on their AWS account (or LocalStack) and Kafka through `docker-compose.kafka.yml`.

## 9v. What M27 built (on the local branch `feat/m27-connectors`, not pushed)

The design is `architecture.md` §2.30 (with "As built"); the step record is §14 (it moves here when the next milestone is planned). The plan (`1cf0a75`) and six steps, built in away mode (the user away), each green before its commit, stacked on `feat/m26-queue-triggers`.

- **The HTTP core** (step 1a `e4158b5`): `packages/connectors/src/http-core.ts` out of `rest.ts` (REST unchanged); `Connector.httpConfig` and `httpConfigFor(type, config)`, so the server's OAuth (M9) and custom auth flows work for any HTTP type.
- **The built-in REST API resource** (step 1b `771fe9e`): `restapi.ts`, `rest_api` seeded by `migrate` (no migration), `REST_API_PRIVATE_HOSTS`, the app viewer's host rule.
- **GraphQL** (step 2 `0dd9246`): `graphql.ts`, `startTestGraphql`, `runOperation`, `resources.graphqlSchema`.
- **Services** (steps 3a `5a35ad3` Slack and the shared base, 3b `e7dac75` HubSpot, 3c `c0f4dd2` SendGrid, 3d `10ad87c` Google Sheets): `services/actions.ts` (`@ow/connectors/actions`), `services/common.ts`, a connector each, `services/fake.ts` (`startFakeServices`).
- **Server** (step 4 `2071a2a`): the services' test URLs, Sheets' server identity (its own scopes; admins only), settings checks, `resources.hubspotProperties`, types grouped, `restapi` not creatable, `ow_connector_queries_total`, `@ow/git-format` `query-text.ts` (`.graphql`, `.html`).
- **Web** (steps 5a–5b `b621551`, 5c `c96bf01`): settings forms (`service-settings.tsx`), the built-in REST API's page, the grouped picker, `components/action-query.tsx` in the inspector, the playground and the app editor; the GraphQL editor on `graphql-language-service` (`components/code/graphql.ts`); collab text keys.
- **Done-when** (step 6a `aa5696d`): `apps/web/e2e/m27-done-when.spec.ts` (2 tests) and `e2e/connectors.spec.ts` (4).
- **Ops** (step 6c): `docs/runbooks/connectors.md`, `REST_API_PRIVATE_HOSTS` in Compose and `.env.example`.
- **New dependencies:** `graphql` 16.14.2 (connectors, server, web), `graphql-language-service` 5.7.0 (web). **Migrations:** none.
- **Review fixes** (session 33, the `code-review` skill (high) on `feat/m26-queue-triggers...feat/m27-connectors`: 10 found, each checked against the code, all held, all fixed in one commit): **the built-in REST API returned its own credentials** (an API key sent as a query parameter came back in `metadata.url`, a bearer token in an echoing API's data; `rest_api` has no resource secrets for `runQuery` to scrub): it now scrubs the request's token, password, basic credentials and key (raw and URL-encoded) from results and errors; **REST's HEAD regressed** on the HTTP core (a 404 threw) and `durationMs` included the body download: both as before; Google Sheets read the header row from `Sheet1` for a bare cell range (now the first sheet's); `ow_connector_queries_total` took labels from unparsed actions (now the parsed query's); SendGrid split `"Doe, John" <…>` at the comma; the 429 wait left a listener on the signal and ignored one already aborted; the app panel kept an SQL query when switching from a missing resource; duplicates removed: `fileValue` (shared by multipart parts and service files), `apiCall` and `answer` (the services' "Call any …" and results; `..` now checked decoded, GET with a body refused), `urlEvaluate`/`renderParams` (REST and the built-in REST API).
- **Totals:** connectors 325 (283 at the end of M26), collab 85, git-format 2445 (2443), server 573 + 1 skipped (556 + 1), web unit 106; e2e: see §15.

## 9w. What M28 built (on the local branch `feat/m28-workflow-editor`, not pushed)

The design is `architecture.md` §2.31 (with "As built"); the step record is §14 (it moves here when the next milestone is planned). The plan (`9c7d621`) and eight steps, stacked on `feat/m27-connectors` (from `3706ad0`), with screenshots before and after each change (a temporary `apps/web/e2e/zz-walkthrough.spec.ts`, never committed).

- **RabbitMQ resource** (step 1 `d4007f4`): `packages/connectors/src/rabbitmq/` (`client.ts`, `connector.ts`: the `publish` action with publisher confirms and mandatory routing), `test-rabbitmq.ts` (`rabbitmq:4.1.4-alpine` in Docker); web `RabbitFields`.
- **RabbitMQ trigger** (step 2 `18ade7e`): `triggers.rabbitmq` in `@ow/definition`, **migration 0035** (`brokers`), `engine/src/queue-adapters/rabbitmq.ts`; the editor's Triggers panel section, `OwRabbitMessage`, the run line.
- **SNS** (step 3 `208773d`): `aws/sns.ts` (`publish`), `aws/fake-sns.ts`; the webhook preset `sns` (`apps/server/src/services/sns-webhook.ts`); web `SnsFields`, the preset's topic ARNs.
- **Kinesis** (step 4 `3b6fb82`, `4d1fa9a`): `aws/kinesis.ts` (`putRecords`), `aws/fake-kinesis.ts`, `triggers.kinesis`, `engine/src/queue-adapters/kinesis.ts` (checkpoints in `kinesis_checkpoints`); web `KinesisFields`, `OwKinesisMessage`.
- **Schema Registry** (step 5 `6d9611a`): `kafka/registry.ts`, `kafka/fake-registry.ts`; Kafka's `schemaRegistry` settings and `schemaRegistryPassword`; produce's `subject` per message; the Kafka adapter's decoding (`schemaId`, `schemaError`); web: the Kafka form's Schema Registry section. Also `bodyData` (`engine/src/queue-adapters/pubsub.ts`): non-text bodies as base64 with `encoding: 'base64'` for every broker (a fix to M19/M26: such bodies were redelivered forever).
- **Done-when and docs** (step 6): `apps/web/e2e/m30-done-when.spec.ts` (global setup: RabbitMQ in Docker, the SNS topic and Kinesis stream on the fake AWS, the fake registry, `/sns/*` and `/rabbit/drain` control routes, `E2E_M30`, `E2E_RABBIT`); runbooks `docs/runbooks/rabbitmq.md`, `kinesis.md`, `sns.md`, and `kafka.md`'s Schema Registry section; the Kafka run line names the schema.
- **New dependencies:** `amqplib` 0.10.9 (MIT) and `@types/amqplib`, `@aws-sdk/client-sns` and `@aws-sdk/client-kinesis` (3.1152.0, Apache-2.0), `@smithy/node-http-handler` (Apache-2.0), `avsc` 5.7.9 (MIT). **Migrations:** 0035. **Environment (tests only):** `AWS_SNS_URL`, `AWS_KINESIS_URL`, `SNS_CERT_HOSTS`.

**Progress:** step 0 `9710f2b`. Step 1 (RabbitMQ resource): `rabbitmq/client.ts` (`rabbitClient`: one connection per configuration, reopened after a close; a confirm channel for publishing; the host's addresses checked against the private-network guard unless allowed; TLS with an optional CA), `rabbitmq/connector.ts` (an **action type** like the services, so the forms come free: `publish` with exchange, routing key, messages; publisher confirms; `mandatory` so a message no queue takes is an error; the channel's close reason (`NOT_FOUND - no exchange …`) reported), `test-rabbitmq.ts`; **amqplib 0.10.9** (MIT; not 2.2.1, published two days earlier) and `@types/amqplib`; the server's password check, the Messaging group; web `RabbitFields`. 4 connector tests, 1 server test. Step 2 (RabbitMQ trigger): `triggers.rabbitmq` in `@ow/definition` (queue any name but `amq.…` or control characters; validation codes `invalid_rabbitmq`, `duplicate_rabbitmq`), **migration 0035** (`brokers`: the kinds' check constraint and run trigger types with `rabbitmq` and `kinesis`, table `kinesis_checkpoints`), `QUEUE_KINDS`/the lease queries, `engine/src/queue-adapters/rabbitmq.ts` (a consumer with the trigger's prefetch, deliveries buffered for `pull`; ack; nack without requeue; handles tied to their channel, so after a lost channel the broker's redelivery wins; ids from `messageId`, else per delivery), `RabbitRunMessage`; the projection's kinds, the workflows router's queue state, the collab spec; the web's Triggers panel section, `OwRabbitMessage` for `startTrigger.message`, the start trigger's summary and the run line. Tests: 4 engine consumer tests (a real broker: ack, dead-lettering, prefetch and stop handing messages back, a missing queue reported then picked up), 2 definition tests, 1 server test (a live release run by the worker from a message). The test helper's `send` waits for confirms (a plain send before closing lost messages). Step 3 (SNS): `aws/sns.ts` (an action type: `publish` to a topic ARN, `PublishBatch` in tens, FIFO needs group ids; M26's identity; `AWS_SNS_URL` for tests), `aws/fake-sns.ts` (topics, PublishBatch in XML or JSON, HTTP subscriptions sent signed SubscriptionConfirmation and Notification envelopes, its RSA key served as the certificate; plugged into the fake AWS, whose unsigned GETs for the certificate and SubscribeURL it answers); the webhook preset **`sns`** (`@ow/definition`: `topicArns` required, no secret; `apps/server/src/services/sns-webhook.ts`: the certificate only from `https://sns.<region>.amazonaws.com/….pem` (or `SNS_CERT_HOSTS`, tests only), cached a day, SignatureVersion 1 and 2, the topic in the list, a SubscriptionConfirmation confirmed by fetching its SubscribeURL (same host rule) and answered without a run, a Notification's run data `{message (JSON parsed), subject, attributes, messageId, topicArn, timestamp}`, the message id as idempotency key; publish skips the secret check); web: the preset's topic ARNs field (no secret), `SnsFields`. Tests: 2 connector, 2 server webhook (confirmation, notifications v1 and v2, a tampered message, a foreign certificate and another topic refused). Step 4 (Kinesis): `aws/kinesis.ts` (an action type: `putRecords` in 500s, a random partition key without one; the reads the adapter needs; **HTTP/1.1** through `@smithy/node-http-handler`, since the SDK's default HTTP/2 handler is only for SubscribeToShard and fails on HTTP/1.1 servers such as the fake), `aws/fake-kinesis.ts` (shards over MD5 hash ranges, iterators, closed shards ending, splits with parent links), `triggers.kinesis` (stream name or ARN, `startAt` latest/trimHorizon, attempts, backoff), `engine/src/queue-adapters/kinesis.ts` (one record per shard at a time; checkpoints in `kinesis_checkpoints` on success and on skipping; a failed record retried before the ones after it, doubling backoff, then `dropped`; a child shard waits for its parent to be read to its end; an expired iterator made again from the checkpoint), `KinesisRunMessage`; projection defaults (latest, 3 attempts, 10 s); web: the Triggers panel's Kinesis section, `OwKinesisMessage`, the run line, `KinesisFields`. Tests: 2 connector, 3 engine (order and restart from the checkpoint, retry then skip, a split), 1 definition, 1 server (a worker runs the live release from a record and saves the checkpoint); the RabbitMQ server test's workflow renamed `on_rabbit_jobs` (it collided with the SQS test's `on_jobs` when the file runs whole). Step 5 (Schema Registry, session 37, 2026-11-04, **built, not committed yet**): `kafka/registry.ts` (Confluent's REST API through the REST client, so basic auth and the private-network guard come free; schemas by id cached, a subject's latest cached a minute; Avro via **avsc 5.7.9**, decoded to plain JSON: bytes as base64; JSON Schema as the JSON after the header; Protobuf refused), Kafka config `schemaRegistry {url, username?, allowPrivateNetwork}` and secret `schemaRegistryPassword` (settings problem: a username needs it), `kafka/fake-registry.ts`; produce: **a `subject` per message** (not the plan's query-level `subject`/`format`: the format comes from the subject's schema type, and one produce can mix subjects), Avro values checked against the schema; the trigger decodes framed values (`message.schemaId`), an undecodable one runs as it came with `message.schemaError`; web: the Kafka form's Schema Registry section, the produce hint, `OwKafkaMessage`'s fields. **A fix found on the way (M19/M26's, all brokers):** a body with a NUL byte (every wire-format value) or not UTF-8 made a run whose insert Postgres's jsonb refused, and the message was redelivered forever (Kafka: its partition's commits stalled); now `bodyData` in `pubsub.ts` gives such bytes as base64 with `message.encoding: 'base64'` (Pub/Sub, RabbitMQ, Kafka, Kinesis; SQS through it for JSON's `\u0000` escapes). Tests: 3 connector (registry), 3 engine (`body-data.test.ts`), 1 engine Kafka (a real broker: an Avro value decoded, an unknown schema as base64 with `schemaError`); the 6 consumer suites 40 passed, server connectors/resources/aws 50 passed. Committed as `6d9611a`. Step 6 (done-when and docs, session 37): `m30-done-when.spec.ts`, 2 tests: (1) SNS and Kinesis: a webhook with the `sns` preset subscribed through the fake (its confirmation fetched, no run), a Kinesis trigger set in the editor from the oldest record, one workflow publishing to the topic and putting into the stream, each delivery running its workflow, the runs page's Kinesis line; (2) with Docker: a RabbitMQ resource made, saved and tested in the UI, a Kafka resource with its Schema Registry in the UI (a username without its password refused), a RabbitMQ trigger set in the editor and a Kafka trigger from the start, one workflow publishing to the queue and producing Avro by subject, both running (the queue and its dead-letter queue empty; the Avro value decoded with its `schemaId`), the runs page's lines (the Kafka one with "· schema N"). Passed twice, alongside `queue-triggers.spec.ts` and `connectors.spec.ts` (10 passed). Docs: the three runbooks, `kafka.md`, architecture's "As built" and the M30 row ✅, §1, §9y. Committed as `03fa35e`. Step 7 (review, session 37): the `code-review` skill (high) over `54e396a..HEAD` found 10; all fixed: **Kinesis** (1) a read failing mid-round lost the records already taken and left their shards waiting for good: they're handed out; (2) `startAt: latest` read a resharded stream's children from their start: a child starts at its beginning only when it carries on from a shard this trigger read, and shards already closed are skipped; (3) a merge's `AdjacentParentShardId` was ignored: a shard waits for all its parents; (4) changing a trigger's stream (or resource, environment) reused the old shards' checkpoints: the release clears them, and the adapter's save only writes while the trigger row still names its stream (`FOR SHARE`, so it waits for such a release); **RabbitMQ** (5) the host's private-address check could be passed by DNS rebinding: `guardedLookup` on the socket too; (6) the default hold (60 min) outlasted RabbitMQ's `consumer_timeout` (30): 25 min for RabbitMQ, the field's hint says why; (7) no `undelivered`: a delivery whose run couldn't start kept a prefetch slot: requeued after 5 s; and `storable` in the consumer turns a NUL in any run input (a header) into U+FFFD, so such a run starts at all; **Kafka** (8) a decode failure stuck to a message's retries: decoded again; **SNS** (9) unauthenticated deliveries could make the server fetch a certificate URL each: in-flight fetches shared, failures remembered 5 min, at most 10 new certificates a minute; (10) the queue kinds repeated as literals: `QUEUE_TRIGGER_KINDS` in `@ow/definition` (the engine's SQL, validate, releases, the web's editor). Tests: 3 engine Kinesis (fake: `merge`, `failNext`), 2 engine RabbitMQ, 1 engine Kafka (fake registry: `down`), `storable`'s, 1 server SNS, the Kinesis release test extended; each new test failed on the code before its fix. Runbooks updated (RabbitMQ's hold and timeout, Kinesis's `latest` and stream changes, SNS's certificate limit). Checks: biome ci; typecheck of every package; definition 193, connectors 380, engine 212, web 125, server 583 (+1 skipped) passed; **full e2e 145 passed, 1 failed**: `webhook-settings.spec.ts`'s body-schema test, a flake from before M30 (on the step 6 build it failed 4 of 5 alone): its `.suggest-widget` locator met the schema editor's hidden widget too (strict mode), or Monaco's worker was slow under the full run; now `.suggest-widget.visible`, 6 of 6 passed.

## 9aa. What M32 built (on `feat/m32-python-packages`)

The design is `architecture.md` §2.35 (with "As built"); the step record is below. Stacked on `feat/m31-git-hosts` (`5752365`). Built in away mode (session 38).

- **A warm pool per env** (step 1 `ba6765e`): `EnvPools` and `env_roots` in `apps/code-executor/executor/server.py`; `test/env-pools.test.ts`.

Step 1 (`EnvPools` in `server.py`, `env_roots` from the env's `*.dist-info`, the runner skipping a root that raises `SystemExit`; `test/env-pools.test.ts`, 4 tests; executor suite 51): measured in the test containers, a run in a package env importing pandas p50 **30 ms warm** (20/20 warm) vs 272 ms cold. Env jails start only when the base pool is full and count towards `PYTHON_WARM_START_PARALLEL`. A root's failed import isn't logged (the jail's stderr is the code's): the jail comes up without it. Step 2 (indexes in `builder.py`: `check_indexes`, credentials through pip's environment (`PIP_INDEX_URL`, `PIP_EXTRA_INDEX_URL`, percent-encoded; **not netrc** as planned: netrc can't hold spaces and keys by host), `attribute` naming each package's index from the extra indexes' pages (by its hash), `/install` in **one** pip call with every index the lock names (the hashes pin each file), `probe` for `/check-index` and to explain a failed build, `hide` for passwords echoed; `PYTHON_ALLOW_INSECURE_INDEXES`; the test index's basic auth; `test/builder-indexes.test.ts`, 7 tests; executor suite 58). Step 3 (sdists: `sdist-jail.cfg`; the builder's image gains `build-essential` and nsjail; nsjail runs **as the builder's user** (a user namespace; no cgroups, which need root: `rlimit_as` `PYTHON_SDIST_MEMORY_MB`, `rlimit_nproc` in the jail's own namespace, the container's 2g); `resolve` loops: pip's "Could not find a version that satisfies the requirement X" → `find_sdist` on the indexes pip searched (pip's vendored `packaging`) → `fetch` (the sign-in only to the index's own host, hash checked) → `build_requirements` (pyproject read from the archive) → `pip download --only-binary` into a wheelhouse → `jail_build` (`pip wheel --no-index`) → `envs/.wheels/<sha>/`, then pip again with `--find-links`; `/install` uses the kept wheel or builds it again (`locked_sdist_wheel`); the cleaner removes kept wheels unused for `PYTHON_ENV_KEEP_DAYS`; test sdists carry their own backend (`buildSdist`, a ustar writer); `test/builder-sdists.test.ts`, 6 tests, the jail probe telling no network by `ENETUNREACH`; executor suite 64. Once, under the full suite's load, `executor.test.ts`'s "counts a warm jail's limits from its request" (1.5 s of CPU in a 2 s timeout) failed; it passed alone and in two more full runs: a possible flake). Step 4 (server: migration **0037** `python_indexes` (slug, name, url, username, `password_config_var` (a config var's **name**, as webhook signatures do), mode) and `python_envs.index_id`; `requirementsHash(requirements, indexId)` keeps M23's keys without an index; `settings.python.index` (definition, `invalid_python_index`; Git: `# index: <slug>` as `requirements.txt`'s first line, since the format keeps `settings.python` out of the JSON); `python-envs.ts`: `pythonIndexBySlug`, `builderIndex` (the password from the default environment), `lockIndexes`; the `pythonIndexes` router (list for everyone without credentials' details, admin create/update (slug fixed; envs of the index dropped)/delete (refused while a draft or live release names it)/test); `workflows.pythonEnv` takes the index and answers `details`; an unknown index fails installs and publishing as `python_packages` (not a save-time problem: the definition can't see the database); `test/python-indexes.test.ts` 4 tests; the package tests' Python blocks get a 60 s timeout and 120 s per test: under the full suite's load the 10 s block timeout (which counts the wait for packages) ran out; full server suite 605 + 1 skipped). Step 5 (web: Settings → Python (`pages/settings/python.tsx`: the indexes, New index/Edit with Test, Delete; admins only; the slug from the name, fixed once made); the workflow's Python packages field gains "From" (the index select, kept when the lines change; an index not on this instance listed as such) and the installed packages' index and "built from source"; Install now installs what the field holds **now** (the done-when caught it installing the 600 ms-settled value, without a just-picked index); shots in `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-optiply-optiply-workflows/b877d319-03d1-41e7-99bf-81415c05d4bf/scratchpad/m32-shots/before` and `/after`; web unit tests 126). Step 6 (`m32-done-when.spec.ts`: Settings → Python's form and Test (refused without the password, then signed in), a workflow picking the index in its Settings, Install now (a package from the index and one built from source), a draft run, the next one warm (the env's package in `sys.modules` before the code imports it), publish and live runs warm, a workflow without the index failing; the e2e setup's private index; the runbook, `.env.example`, §2.35 "As built", §9aa). M23's done-when failed once while running beside the M32 one (a run failed; not kept); alone it passed with the Python specs. Step 7 (the review, `code-review` high: 10 found, all fixed: **a deadlock** between the base pool's and the env pools' maintenance threads (each read the other's counts under its own lock; now read before taking it); **an index's sign-in following a redirect to another host** (now an unredirected header, kept by `SignedRedirects` only on the index's host, never https→http); attribution guessing when an index's page failed (now matched by the download URL's prefix, else pages read strictly: a failure fails the build); relative sdist links resolved against the requested URL, not the one served; idle env jails' memory (`PYTHON_WARM_ENV_MAX_MB`, 256: a heavier one isn't kept, `tooBig` in `/health`); a wheel built from an sdist not checked against its project and version (`check_built`; pip's own `wheel` refuses it first in the test); a 404 index root taken for a good sign-in (Test now fails it, saying GitLab answers 404 to a wrong sign-in; a failed build doesn't blame it); a broken kept-wheel folder crashing the build (replaced now); Install now hiding an unrecorded failure behind a stale "Installed"; attribution's page per package (now only when the URL doesn't tell). ## 10. How things were verified (and how to repeat it)

- **Unit and integration tests:** `pnpm test`. Each server, db, connector and engine Postgres test file boots its own embedded Postgres. The engine suite was run three times in a row after step 3 with no flakes.
- **Webhook latency:** `pnpm --filter @ow/server bench:webhooks` (prints p50/p90/p99).
- **Sandbox context speed:** `pnpm --filter @ow/sandbox bench` (0.66 ms locally).
- **Load:** at load averages of 10–30 (all 13 suites at once while macOS indexes, Chrome, VS Code and Slack run) timing tests fail that pass alone: sandbox timers, code-executor speed and limits, an engine loop batch, m9's scheduled run, app-sandbox's restart. Rerun the package alone (`pnpm turbo run test --filter <pkg> --concurrency=1`) before suspecting code; one such failure was a real bug (`b06f4ff`). Turbo stopping a suite midway can leave `mysql:8.4` and `ow-code-executor-test-*` containers running: remove them by name.
- **Playwright e2e (committed):** `pnpm --filter @ow/web build && pnpm --filter @ow/server build && pnpm --filter @ow/web e2e`. Locally it uses the installed Chrome (`channel: 'chrome'`); CI uses Playwright's Chromium. The server runs on port 4387 (`E2E_PORT`). Fifteen specs at the end of M8 (nine after M7); 27 tests in 20 files with M12's Python spec (it needs the executor container: Docker). For repeats, pass `--repeat-each=3` to `pnpm exec playwright test` in `apps/web` (through `pnpm … e2e -- …` the flag doesn't reach Playwright). The `typeInto` helper clicks a Monaco field near its top-left corner: with more lines than fit, the middle of `.view-lines` is over the next field.
- **MySQL tests** start a `mysql:8.4` container (Docker) or use `MYSQL_TEST_URL`; skipped locally without Docker, required in CI (~46 s for the connector file, including the container start).

- **Docker Compose, session 4** (architecture §2.8; scripts in the session scratchpad, not committed):
  - `docker compose up -d --build` with `PUBLIC_URL=http://localhost:3000`; admin via `docker compose exec api node dist/cli/create-admin.js`.
  - Databases `shop_prod` and `shop_staging` in the Compose Postgres with different `orders`; a `shop` Postgres resource configured for both (test connection OK in both).
  - The `orders` workflow (webhook → query → JS → branch → two Responses), published v1.0.0: `curl` with `X-Api-Key` returned the production answer, `?environment=staging` (through the alias `customers/:customerId/orders`) the staging one; no key → 401; `Prefer: respond-async` → 202 + `Location`; the same `Idempotency-Key` twice → the same run, replayed.
  - **`kill -9` of a worker mid-run:** a live run of `a (3 s) → b (25 s) → c` on a worker; while `b` ran, `docker kill -s KILL` on the container holding the lease. The other worker resumed it once the 30 s lease expired; the run succeeded with 1 recovery and epoch 3; `a` ran once (not re-run), `b` re-ran (its first insert rolled back with the killed connection). Note: `docker kill` counts as a manual stop, so `restart: unless-stopped` doesn't restart the container; `docker compose up -d worker` does.
  - **1-minute cron, 2-minute run:** the `slow-cron` workflow (`insert` a hit, then `select pg_sleep(120)`, block timeout 150 s) with schedule `* * * * *`, overlap `skip`, watched for 15 minutes through the database: runs fired at 09:01, 09:04, 09:07, 09:10 and 09:13 (each 120 s; the fire at the minute a run ends is checked just before it finishes, so one starts every 3 minutes), 10 fires were logged as skipped, and a self-join on the runs' start and finish times found **0 overlapping pairs**. The first three runs failed with "statement timeout": a Postgres resource's `statementTimeoutMs` defaults to 120 s and caps the block timeout (M1); after raising it to 180 s, the next two succeeded. Then v1.0.1 turned the schedule off (its trigger kept its id, `next_fire_at` null).
- **Bundled server without Docker:** `node --no-node-snapshot dist/main.js` with `MIGRATIONS_DIR=../../packages/db/migrations` (and `WEB_DIST_DIR=../web/dist` to serve the UI). Restart it after rebuilding the web app: `@fastify/static` lists files once at startup, and stale asset names return the SPA's HTML (a MIME-type error in the browser).
- **Browser walkthroughs** (sessions 3–6, not committed): session 3 used puppeteer-core scripts; sessions 4–6 used a temporary Playwright spec (`e2e/zz-walkthrough.spec.ts`, deleted afterwards) reusing `e2e/global-setup.ts` with screenshots read back (session 6: the MySQL and BigQuery forms, the Inbox, the task form, the User Task form and the audit page). Gotchas:
  - Typing into Monaco races its auto-closing on slow machines: use `keyboard.insertText` (see `e2e/helpers.ts`).
  - Monaco wraps long lines on screen: rendered text contains extra line breaks.
  - Click empty canvas away from the floating toolbar (top left), controls (bottom left) and minimap (bottom right).
  - "Not published" also matches a button named "Publish": use `exact: true`.
  - A field label containing "host" (e.g. the SSL option "…hostname") matches `getByLabel('Host')`: prefer `getByRole('textbox', { name })`. Fields inside a `Field` `<label>` take its hint into their accessible name; buttons inside one take the whole label (the label editors' "Add").

## 11. Local environment quirks (this Mac)

- **Optiply's own API as a REST resource** (tried by the user in session 18): use the base URL **`https://api.optiply.com/v1/`** with the trailing slash. Without it, `GET /v1` answers 302 to an internal hostname (`http://public-api-node-2.public-api.svc.cluster.local/v1/`, from its Istio gateway), which the resource rightly refuses (Test connection fails `blocked`). Queries with a path aren't affected. Worth reporting to Optiply's API team. The API is JSON:API: send `Accept: application/vnd.api+json`; a 404 "Resource not found." is a record the token can't see.
- **The Compose stack** runs M17 (session 20: `fc35ec1` with `9840ec7`'s change, built from a clean worktree), which includes M16's review fixes and `41b7408`'s. It was built from a clean worktree (`docker build -t optiply-workflows:local .` there, then `PUBLIC_URL=http://localhost:3000 docker compose up -d`) so uncommitted files in the tree stay out. Older images: `optiply-workflows:m15-backup` (M15) and `m14-backup` (M14); `m16-check` (session 19) is kept as the user asked. A second API process for checks needs its own `PUBLIC_URL` (the collab socket checks the Origin).
- Node v24.15.0 (arm64). **Docker Desktop 29.8 with Compose v5.6 is installed** (since session 4; 8 CPUs, about 4 GB for Docker). No global pnpm, no system Postgres.
- Run pnpm as **`COREPACK_ENABLE_DOWNLOAD_PROMPT=0 corepack pnpm …`**. **Turbo needs a `pnpm` binary on PATH.** In a Claude session, run `corepack enable --install-directory <scratchpad>/bin pnpm` and prepend that directory to PATH. The user can simply run `corepack enable`.
- **Dev database:** `pnpm dev:db` stores its data in `.data/pg` (gitignored), with `DATABASE_URL=postgres://ow:ow@127.0.0.1:5433/optiply_workflows`. It has a `demo_customers` table, a "Demo DB" resource (`demo_db`), and test workflows from session 3, all safe to delete; it's still at migration `0003` and migrates on the next `pnpm dev`.
- **Compose stack, end of session 14:** the image of `feat/optiply-ui` at `c00c6d8` (step 6, **before the review fixes**: no minimap button, chart and code colours still hex copies); `api` and `worker` recreated with `--no-deps`, Postgres and its data untouched. The same database as below. To bring it to `main`: `PUBLIC_URL=http://localhost:3000 docker compose build api && PUBLIC_URL=http://localhost:3000 docker compose up -d --no-deps api worker` (check the user isn't using it first).
- **Compose stack, end of session 13:** the image of `feat/m13-editor-polish` at `767222b` (M13 steps 1–4; rebuilt for the Compose check, §10). The user's `test` workflow and a resource of theirs on it; `m13-check@optiply.nl` disabled.
- **Compose stack: Docker Desktop was reset to factory in session 13** (after an error the user couldn't capture). That deleted every image and the `optiply-workflows_pgdata` volume, and there was no backup, so everything the history below describes is gone (the `admin@optiply.nl` and `m7-check` admins, the user's `test` workflow, API key and `cancel-me` schedule, the managed database's contents). It was rebuilt from `main` at `13ea53e` with `PUBLIC_URL=http://localhost:3000 docker compose up --build -d`: migrations from scratch, the managed database re-provisioned (`owdb_production`, `owdb_staging`), the code executor, Google SSO on. Its only user is **`fabioceriaco@optiply.nl`** (admin as the first user, signed in with Google; also has a local password). **Always start or recreate it with `PUBLIC_URL=http://localhost:3000`**: Compose reads `.env`, whose `PUBLIC_URL` is Vite's `:5173`, and the Google callback is built from it. Docker Desktop's memory is the factory 4 GB.
- **The Compose stack before the reset** (volume `optiply-workflows_pgdata`): it ran the image of **`feat/m12-python`** at `ec7280b` (rebuilt in session 12 with the user's OK), **with the `code-executor` service** and **`PYTHON_EXECUTOR_TOKEN` in `.env`** (added in session 12; every `docker compose` command, even `ps`, refuses to run without it). Python works on it. Session 12's check left nothing behind (§10); `m7-check`'s password is in session 12's scratchpad. Before that: the image of **`feat/m11-git-sync`** (step 6, uncommitted at build time but the same code as the step 6 commit; rebuilt in session 11 with the user's OK; **migrations 0014–0016 applied**, so going back to `main`'s image runs it on a newer schema: additive tables and columns only). Git sync isn't set up on it. Session 11's check left nothing behind (§10); `m7-check`'s password is in session 11's scratchpad. Before that: the image of `main` at `bb7f6b7` (M10 with its review fixes; migration `0013`). Session 10's check left nothing behind (§10); `m7-check`'s password is in session 10's scratchpad. Before that: the image of `main` at `a04c68e` (M9 with the PR #11 review fixes; no OAuth connections stored). Before that: the M9 step 7 image (session 9's Compose check). Also holds an admin `m7-check@optiply.nl` used by the session 7 and 8 checks (its password was reset again in session 9, kept in that session's scratchpad at `compose/m7-check-password`; reset it again with `create-admin` and `ADMIN_PASSWORD`). Before that: the M6 step 7 image. Holds session 5's `shop` database and resource, the draft workflows `loop-10k`, `wait-restart` and `cancel-me`, and **the user's own work from session 6** (a `test` workflow, an API key, and a schedule on `cancel-me` that fires: check with the user before changing them). Admin `admin@optiply.nl`: the password was reset in session 6 to a random one kept in that session's scratchpad (`compose/admin-password`); set your own with `docker compose exec api node --no-node-snapshot dist/cli/create-admin.js --email admin@optiply.nl --name Admin`. Compose reads `.env`, whose `PUBLIC_URL` points at Vite (`:5173`): run it with `PUBLIC_URL=http://localhost:3000` (or change `.env`). It holds port 3000, like `pnpm dev`. Postgres isn't published to the host: use `docker compose exec postgres psql -U ow -d optiply_workflows`.
- **Don't kill side processes inside the Postgres container** (see the runbook): Postgres is PID 1 there and crash-restarts when an adopted child dies by signal.
- **The session scratchpad can be cleared mid-session** (it happened in session 6): recreate the `pnpm` shim with `corepack enable --install-directory <scratchpad>/bin pnpm`.
- **isolated-vm compiles on `pnpm install`** (the patch): needs the Xcode command-line tools.
